<p><b>Príncipe Island</b>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Autonomous_Region_of_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Autonomous Region of Príncipe">Pagué</a></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Economy of São Tomé and Príncipe">Economy of São Tomé and Príncipe</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sao_Tome_and_Principe_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Sao_Tome_and_Principe_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Sao_Tome_and_Principe_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Sao_Tome_and_Principe_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Sao_Tome_and_Principe_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Sao_Tome_and_Principe_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Sao_Tome_and_Principe_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>A proportional representation of São Tomé and Príncipe exports, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture">Agriculture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Agriculture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Since the 19th century, the economy of São Tomé and Príncipe has been based on plantation agriculture. At the time of independence, Portuguese-owned plantations occupied 90% of the cultivated area. After independence, control of these plantations passed to various state-owned agricultural enterprises. The main crop on São Tomé is cocoa, representing about 95% of agricultural exports. Other export crops include copra, palm kernels, and coffee.
</p><p>Domestic food-crop production is inadequate to meet local consumption, so the country imports most of its food.<sup id="cite_ref-AMD_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AMD-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> As of 1997, an estimated 90% of the country's food needs were met through imports.<sup id="cite_ref-AMD_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AMD-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Efforts have been made by the government in recent years to expand food production, and several projects have been undertaken, largely financed by foreign donors.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Other than agriculture, the main economic activities are fishing and a small industrial sector engaged in processing local agricultural products and producing a few basic consumer goods. The scenic islands have potential for tourism, and the government is attempting to improve its rudimentary tourist industry infrastructure. The government sector accounts for about 11% of employment.
</p><p>Following independence, the country had a centrally directed economy, with most means of production owned and controlled by the state. The original constitution guaranteed a <a href="/wiki/Mixed_economy" title="Mixed economy">mixed economy</a>, with privately owned cooperatives combined with publicly owned property and means of production.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_measures">Government measures</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Government measures">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the 1980s and 1990s, the economy of São Tomé encountered major difficulties. Economic growth stagnated, and cocoa exports dropped in both value and volume, creating large balance-of-payments deficits. Plantation land was seized, resulting in the complete collapse of cocoa production. At the same time, the international price of cocoa slumped.
</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:2017_Guadalupe_STP_DSC_8344_(32064969063).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/2017_Guadalupe_STP_DSC_8344_%2832064969063%29.jpg/300px-2017_Guadalupe_STP_DSC_8344_%2832064969063%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="110" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/2017_Guadalupe_STP_DSC_8344_%2832064969063%29.jpg/450px-2017_Guadalupe_STP_DSC_8344_%2832064969063%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/2017_Guadalupe_STP_DSC_8344_%2832064969063%29.jpg/600px-2017_Guadalupe_STP_DSC_8344_%2832064969063%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4595" data-file-height="1687" /></a><figcaption>Oil and gas infrastructure in Neves, São Tomé and Príncipe</figcaption></figure>
<p>In response to its economic downturn, the government undertook a series of far-reaching economic reforms. In 1987, the government implemented an <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> <a href="/wiki/Structural_adjustment" title="Structural adjustment">structural adjustment</a> program, and invited greater private participation in management of the <a href="/wiki/State-owned_enterprise" title="State-owned enterprise">parastatals</a>, as well as in the agricultural, commercial, banking, and tourism sectors. The focus of economic reform since the early 1990s has been widespread privatization, especially of the state-run agricultural and industrial sectors.
</p><p>The São Toméan government has traditionally obtained foreign assistance from various donors, including the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme" title="United Nations Development Programme">UN Development Programme</a>, the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, the European Union, Portugal, <a href="/wiki/Taiwan" title="Taiwan">Taiwan</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/African_Development_Bank" title="African Development Bank">African Development Bank</a>. In April 2000, in association with the <a href="/wiki/Central_Bank_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Central Bank of São Tomé and Príncipe">Banco Central de São Tomé e Príncipe</a>, the IMF approved a poverty-reduction and growth facility for São Tomé aimed at reducing inflation to 3% for 2001, raising ideal growth to 4%, and reducing the fiscal deficit.
</p><p>In late 2000, São Tomé qualified for significant debt reduction under the IMF–World Bank's <a href="/wiki/Heavily_Indebted_Poor_Countries" class="mw-redirect" title="Heavily Indebted Poor Countries">Heavily Indebted Poor Countries</a> initiative. The reduction is being reevaluated by the IMF, due to the attempted <i>coup d'état</i> in July 2003 and subsequent emergency spending. Following the truce, the IMF decided to send a mission to São Tomé to evaluate the macroeconomic state of the country. This evaluation is ongoing, reportedly pending oil legislation to determine how the government will manage incoming oil revenues, which are still poorly defined, but in any case expected to change the economic situation dramatically.
</p><p>In parallel, some efforts have been made to incentivize private tourism initiatives, but their scope remains limited.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>São Tomé also hosts a broadcasting station of the American <a href="/wiki/International_Broadcasting_Bureau" title="International Broadcasting Bureau">International Broadcasting Bureau</a> for the <a href="/wiki/Voice_of_America" title="Voice of America">Voice of America</a><sup id="cite_ref-WRTH1995p162_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WRTH1995p162-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> at Pinheira.<sup id="cite_ref-WRTH1997p514_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WRTH1997p514-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Portugal remains one of São Tomé's major trading partners, particularly as a source of imports. Food, manufactured articles, machinery, and transportation equipment are imported primarily from the EU.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Economic_challenges">Economic challenges</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Economic challenges">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the past few years, the economy of São Tomé and Príncipe has grown, driven by <a href="/wiki/Agriculture" title="Agriculture">agriculture</a>, tourism and <a href="/wiki/Foreign_investments" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign investments">foreign investments</a>, but mainly grew due to government spending driven by foreign loans. Gross domestic product (GDP) grew at an average rate of 5.5% between 2009 and 2017, but has slowed since 2014. The slowdown in economic growth was caused by lower government spending due to decreased foreign loans and decreased revenue government tax.<sup id="cite_ref-Overview_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Overview-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Palmiers_%C3%A0_huile_%C3%A0_Ribeira_Peixe_(S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9)_(2).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Palmiers_%C3%A0_huile_%C3%A0_Ribeira_Peixe_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%282%29.jpg/300px-Palmiers_%C3%A0_huile_%C3%A0_Ribeira_Peixe_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%282%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="200" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Palmiers_%C3%A0_huile_%C3%A0_Ribeira_Peixe_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%282%29.jpg/450px-Palmiers_%C3%A0_huile_%C3%A0_Ribeira_Peixe_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%282%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Palmiers_%C3%A0_huile_%C3%A0_Ribeira_Peixe_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%282%29.jpg/600px-Palmiers_%C3%A0_huile_%C3%A0_Ribeira_Peixe_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%282%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5472" data-file-height="3648" /></a><figcaption>Palm Tree Plantations in Ribeira Peixe</figcaption></figure>
<p>The biggest challenges for the economy of São Tomé and Príncipe are limited workforce, the fact that São Tomé and Príncipe is an archipelago, a small domestic market, climatic fluctuations, global warming, scarce diplomatic resources and poverty.<sup id="cite_ref-Overview_70-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Overview-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For long-term economic growth, the government is trying to stimulate various sectors of the economy, diversify the economy, cut government spending and encourage private sector and foreign investment.<sup id="cite_ref-:8_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Positive_aspects">Positive aspects</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Positive aspects">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>São Tomé and Príncipe outperforms the sub-Saharan Africa average on the  <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a> and has made great progress on most social indicators. All children in São Tomé and Príncipe are enrolled in the <a href="/wiki/Education_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Education system">education system</a>, <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">life expectancy</a> has increased to 70 years, the <a href="/wiki/Infant_Mortality_Rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Infant Mortality Rate">infant mortality rate</a> has decreased dramatically and the vast majority of the population already has access to piped water and access to electricity.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In terms of business, the government of São Tomé and Príncipe has passed several laws that facilitate the creation of private businesses and foreign investments. Between 2015 and 2019 the number of businesses and small businesses increased greatly.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="This term requires quantification. (September 2021)">quantify</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> This has led to a decrease in unemployment, an increase in exports and the creation of several manufacturers. In the coming years significant economic improvements are expected.<sup id="cite_ref-:8_71-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The tourism sector has great potential to be a way of diversifying the country's economy. This sector has been expanding with the increase of foreign investment. Large resorts have been built on the beaches of São Tomé and Príncipe.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_72-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:S._Tom%C3%A9_2_077_(2269887268).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/S._Tom%C3%A9_2_077_%282269887268%29.jpg/300px-S._Tom%C3%A9_2_077_%282269887268%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/S._Tom%C3%A9_2_077_%282269887268%29.jpg/450px-S._Tom%C3%A9_2_077_%282269887268%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/S._Tom%C3%A9_2_077_%282269887268%29.jpg/600px-S._Tom%C3%A9_2_077_%282269887268%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption>Resort Pestana Equador, São Tomé and Príncipe</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The main <a href="/wiki/Port" title="Port">ports</a> in the country are in the city of São Tomé and Neves, both on the island of São Tomé, which were very degraded before being modernized in 2014. Close to the city of São Tomé, the <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_International_Airport" title="São Tomé International Airport">international airport</a> was expanded and modernized. The telephone system and the road network are good by African standards. Cellphones are widely used and improving. The <a href="/wiki/Internet" title="Internet">Internet service</a> is available and has been widely installed in urban areas.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_72-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:8_71-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Petroleum_exploration">Petroleum exploration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Petroleum exploration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2001, São Tomé and Nigeria reached agreement on joint exploration for petroleum in waters claimed by the two countries of the <a href="/wiki/Niger_Delta_province" class="mw-redirect" title="Niger Delta province">Niger Delta geologic province</a>. After a lengthy series of negotiations, in April 2003, the joint development zone (JDZ) was opened for bids by international oil firms. The JDZ was divided into nine blocks; the winning bids for block one, <a href="/wiki/ChevronTexaco" class="mw-redirect" title="ChevronTexaco">ChevronTexaco</a>, <a href="/wiki/ExxonMobil" title="ExxonMobil">ExxonMobil</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Dangote_Group" title="Dangote Group">Dangote Group</a>, were announced in April 2004,<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> with São Tomé to take in 40% of the $123&#160;million bid, and Nigeria the other 60%. Bids on other blocks were still under consideration in October 2004. São Tomé has received more than $2 million from the bank to develop its petroleum sector.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Banking">Banking</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Banking">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:S%C3%A3o_Tome_DSC_7995_(32036163784).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/S%C3%A3o_Tome_DSC_7995_%2832036163784%29.jpg/300px-S%C3%A3o_Tome_DSC_7995_%2832036163784%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="199" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/S%C3%A3o_Tome_DSC_7995_%2832036163784%29.jpg/450px-S%C3%A3o_Tome_DSC_7995_%2832036163784%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/S%C3%A3o_Tome_DSC_7995_%2832036163784%29.jpg/600px-S%C3%A3o_Tome_DSC_7995_%2832036163784%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4928" data-file-height="3264" /></a><figcaption>Banco Internacional de São Tomé e Príncipe</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Central_Bank_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Central Bank of São Tomé and Príncipe">Banco Central de Sāo Tomé e Príncipe</a> is the central bank, responsible for monetary policy and bank supervision. Six banks are in the country; the largest and oldest is <a href="/wiki/Banco_Internacional_de_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_e_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Banco Internacional de São Tomé e Príncipe">Banco Internacional de São Tomé e Príncipe</a>, which is a subsidiary of Portugal's government-owned <a href="/wiki/Caixa_Geral_de_Dep%C3%B3sitos" title="Caixa Geral de Depósitos">Caixa Geral de Depósitos</a>. It had a monopoly on commercial banking until a change in the banking law in 2003 led to the entry of several other banks.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Business_partners">Business partners</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Business partners">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Exports">Exports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Exports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 2018, exports from São Tomé and Príncipe totaled 24 million euros, an increase of 118% in 5 years, as in 2013 exports from São Tomé and Príncipe totaled only 11 million euros. Half of São Tomé and Príncipe's exports are <a href="/wiki/Cocoa_bean" title="Cocoa bean">cocoa beans</a>. One fifth of exports are electrical machines. Other considerable exports are parts of airplanes, cars, iron, plastics, agricultural products (pepper, oils, nuts and beef).<sup id="cite_ref-:9_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:10_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The main destinations for exports from São Tomé and Príncipe are <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">Europe</a>, where the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands" title="Netherlands">Netherlands</a> (19%), <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a> (14%), <a href="/wiki/Poland" title="Poland">Poland</a> (13%), <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> (7%) and <a href="/wiki/Germany" title="Germany">Germany</a> (6%) stand out. Others important buyers are <a href="/wiki/Singapore" title="Singapore">Singapore</a>, <a href="/wiki/Japan" title="Japan">Japan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a> and the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:9_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_most_important_import_partners_of_Sao_Tome_and_Principe_(2018).png" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="The most important import partners of Sao Tome and Principe (2018)" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/The_most_important_import_partners_of_Sao_Tome_and_Principe_%282018%29.png/249px-The_most_important_import_partners_of_Sao_Tome_and_Principe_%282018%29.png" decoding="async" width="249" height="234" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/The_most_important_import_partners_of_Sao_Tome_and_Principe_%282018%29.png/374px-The_most_important_import_partners_of_Sao_Tome_and_Principe_%282018%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/The_most_important_import_partners_of_Sao_Tome_and_Principe_%282018%29.png/498px-The_most_important_import_partners_of_Sao_Tome_and_Principe_%282018%29.png 2x" data-file-width="809" data-file-height="761" /></a><figcaption>The most important import partners of São Tomé and Príncipe (2018)</figcaption></figure>
<p>In the last 10 years, the countries in which the value of exports increased the most were <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Poland" title="Poland">Poland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands" title="Netherlands">Netherlands</a>. There was a sharp decrease in exports from São Tomé and Príncipe to <a href="/wiki/Angola" title="Angola">Angola</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mexico" title="Mexico">Mexico</a> and <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:9_75-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Imports">Imports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Imports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 2018, imports to São Tomé and Príncipe totalled 161 million dollars. Since 2013, imports have been decreasing, albeit at a slow pace, since in 2013 imports totalled 167 million euros. A fifth of imports to São Tomé and Príncipe corresponded to <a href="/wiki/Petroleum_product" title="Petroleum product">refined oil</a> (mainly from Angola). Other important imports, in order of importance, were cars, rice, cereals, <a href="/wiki/Wine" title="Wine">wine</a>, electronic equipment, chemicals, clothing, meat, medical equipment, and wood.<sup id="cite_ref-:9_75-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>About 51%, more than half of the imports of São Tomé and Príncipe come from <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>. A fifth of imports come from <a href="/wiki/Angola" title="Angola">Angola</a>, about 6% come from <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a>, 4% from the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>, 4% from <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a>, 2% from <a href="/wiki/Gabon" title="Gabon">Gabon</a> and 2% from <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:9_75-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the last 10 years, the value of imports increased most from the countries of <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Angola" title="Angola">Angola</a> and <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a>. There was a sharp decrease in imports from <a href="/wiki/Thailand" title="Thailand">Thailand</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nigeria" title="Nigeria">Nigeria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:9_75-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Portugal_2">Portugal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Portugal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>São Tomé and Príncipe imports mostly machines, mainly electric generators and computers, and food, mainly wine, wheat, rice, milk, and soy oil, from Portugal. In addition, São Tomé and Príncipe also imports considerable quantities of cars, soap, and iron from Portugal. Portugal mainly buys scrap material, copper, cocoa, and clothing.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:10_76-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Society">Society</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Society">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Demographics of São Tomé and Príncipe">Demographics of São Tomé and Príncipe</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dimanche_en_famille_sur_la_plage_des_Tamarins_(S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9)_(4).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Dimanche_en_famille_sur_la_plage_des_Tamarins_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%284%29.jpg/220px-Dimanche_en_famille_sur_la_plage_des_Tamarins_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%284%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Dimanche_en_famille_sur_la_plage_des_Tamarins_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%284%29.jpg/330px-Dimanche_en_famille_sur_la_plage_des_Tamarins_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%284%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Dimanche_en_famille_sur_la_plage_des_Tamarins_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%284%29.jpg/440px-Dimanche_en_famille_sur_la_plage_des_Tamarins_%28S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9%29_%284%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5472" data-file-height="3648" /></a><figcaption>Santomeans on the beach, Lobata District</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe.png/360px-S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe.png" decoding="async" width="360" height="212" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe.png/540px-S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe.png/720px-S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe.png 2x" data-file-width="724" data-file-height="426" /></a><figcaption>São Tomé and Príncipe's population in thousands between 1961 and 2003</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tchiloli_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_(56).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Tchiloli_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%2856%29.jpg/220px-Tchiloli_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%2856%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Tchiloli_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%2856%29.jpg/330px-Tchiloli_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%2856%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Tchiloli_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%2856%29.jpg/440px-Tchiloli_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%2856%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5472" data-file-height="3648" /></a><figcaption>A scene of Santomean <i>Tchioli</i>, Auto de Floripes Festival</figcaption></figure>
<p>The total population is estimated at 201,800 in May 2018 by the government agency.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> About 193,380 people live on São Tomé and 8,420 on Príncipe. The natural population increase is about 4,000 people per year.
</p><p>Nearly all citizens are descended from people from different countries taken to the islands by the Portuguese from 1470 onwards. In the 1970s, two significant population movements occurred — the exodus of most of the 4,000 Portuguese residents and the influx of several hundred São Tomé refugees from Angola.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_groups">Ethnic groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Ethnic groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Distinct ethnic groups on São Tomé and Príncipe include:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Luso-Africans" title="Luso-Africans">Luso-Africans</a>, or mixed-blood, are descendants of Portuguese colonists and African slaves brought to the islands during the early years of settlement from <a href="/wiki/Benin" title="Benin">Benin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gabon" title="Gabon">Gabon</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_the_Congo" title="Republic of the Congo">Republic of the Congo</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo" title="Democratic Republic of the Congo">Democratic Republic of the Congo</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Angola" title="Angola">Angola</a> (these people also are known as <i>filhos da terra</i> or "children of the land").</li>
<li><i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Angolares&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Angolares (page does not exist)">Angolares</a></i> are reputedly descendants of Angolan slaves who survived a 1540 shipwreck and now earn their livelihood fishing.</li>
<li><i>Forros</i> are descendants of freed slaves when slavery was abolished.</li>
<li><i>Serviçais</i> are contract laborers from Angola, <a href="/wiki/Mozambique" title="Mozambique">Mozambique</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Cape_Verde" title="Cape Verde">Cape Verde</a>, living temporarily on the islands.</li>
<li><i>Tongas</i> are children of<i> serviçais</i> born on the islands.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europeans</a>, primarily Portuguese</li>
<li>Asians, mostly <a href="/wiki/Overseas_Chinese" title="Overseas Chinese">Chinese</a>, including <a href="/wiki/Macanese_people" title="Macanese people">Macanese people</a> of mixed Portuguese and Chinese descent from <a href="/wiki/Macau" title="Macau">Macau</a></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Languages of São Tomé and Príncipe">Languages of São Tomé and Príncipe</a></div><p><a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a> is the official and the <i>de facto</i> national language of São Tomé and Príncipe, with about 98.4% speaking it, a significant share as their native language, and it has been spoken in the islands since the end of the 15th century. Restructured variants of Portuguese or <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_creole" class="mw-redirect" title="Portuguese creole">Portuguese creoles</a> are also spoken: <a href="/wiki/Forro_Creole" title="Forro Creole">Forro</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Creole_language" title="Creole language">creole language</a> (36.2%), <a href="/wiki/Cape_Verdean_Creole" title="Cape Verdean Creole">Cape Verdean Creole</a> (8.5%), <a href="/wiki/Angolar_Creole" title="Angolar Creole">Angolar</a> (6.6%), and <a href="/wiki/Principense_Creole" title="Principense Creole">Principense</a> (1%). <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> (6.8%) and <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a> (4.9%) are foreign languages taught in schools.
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Religion in São Tomé and Príncipe">Religion in São Tomé and Príncipe</a></div>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">
<div class="mw-no-invert" style="background-color:white;margin:auto;position:relative;width:200px;height:200px;overflow:hidden;border-radius:100px;border:1px solid black;transform:scaleX(-1)rotate(-90deg)">
<div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:0; top:0; border-width:0 200px 200px 0; border-color:LimeGreen"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 47.826161647115px; border-left-color:Yellow"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Yellow"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Yellow"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;right:100px; top:100px; border-width:98.109051744333px 19.354946805086px 0 0; border-top-color:Orange"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Orange"></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religion in São Tomé and Príncipe <sup id="cite_ref-Pew_Forum_-_Religion_in_São_Tomé_and_Principe_(2010)_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pew_Forum_-_Religion_in_São_Tomé_and_Principe_(2010)-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Orange; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> (71.9%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Yellow; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other <a href="/wiki/Christians" title="Christians">Christian</a> (10.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:LimeGreen; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other or Non Religious (17.9%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>The majority of residents belongs to the local branch of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a>, which in turn retains close ties with the church in Portugal. Sizeable <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> minorities of <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church" title="Seventh-day Adventist Church">Seventh-day Adventists</a> and other <a href="/wiki/Evangelical_Protestants" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelical Protestants">Evangelical Protestants</a> exist, as well as a small but growing <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Muslim</a> population.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pico_Papagaio_(20504263334).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Pico_Papagaio_%2820504263334%29.jpg/220px-Pico_Papagaio_%2820504263334%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Pico_Papagaio_%2820504263334%29.jpg/330px-Pico_Papagaio_%2820504263334%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Pico_Papagaio_%2820504263334%29.jpg/440px-Pico_Papagaio_%2820504263334%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption>Nossa Senhora do Rosário Church, in <a href="/wiki/Santo_Ant%C3%B3nio" title="Santo António">Santo António</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Casa_da_Cultura_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_(1).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Casa_da_Cultura_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%281%29.jpg/220px-Casa_da_Cultura_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%281%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="139" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Casa_da_Cultura_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%281%29.jpg/330px-Casa_da_Cultura_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%281%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Casa_da_Cultura_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%281%29.jpg/440px-Casa_da_Cultura_%C3%A0_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_%281%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5238" data-file-height="3312" /></a><figcaption><i>Casa da Cultura</i>, São Tomé and Príncipe</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>See <a href="/wiki/Health_in_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Health in São Tomé and Príncipe">Health in São Tomé and Príncipe</a>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Among sub-Saharan African countries, São Tomé and Príncipe has one of the highest literacy rates.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> According to The World Factbook - Central Intelligence Agency as of 2018, 92.8% of the population age 15 and over can read and write in São Tomé and Príncipe were respectively literate.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_79-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> The Human Rights Measurement Initiative (HRMI)<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> finds that Sao Tome and Principe is fulfilling only 83.8% of what it should be fulfilling for the right to education based on the country's level of income.<sup id="cite_ref-rightstracker.org_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rightstracker.org-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> HRMI breaks down the right to education by looking at the rights to both primary education and secondary education. While taking into consideration Sao Tome and Principe's income level, the nation is achieving 90.4% of what should be possible based on its resources (income) for primary education but only 77.2% for secondary education.<sup id="cite_ref-rightstracker.org_81-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rightstracker.org-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Education in São Tomé and Príncipe is compulsory for six years.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Primary school enrollment and attendance rates were unavailable for São Tomé and Príncipe as of 2001.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_82-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The educational system has a shortage of classrooms, insufficiently trained and underpaid teachers, inadequate textbooks and materials, high rates of repetition, poor educational planning and management, and a lack of community involvement in school management.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_82-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Domestic financing of the school system is lacking, leaving the system highly dependent on foreign financing.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_82-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Tertiary institutions are the <a href="/wiki/National_Lyceum" title="National Lyceum">National Lyceum</a> and the <a href="/wiki/University_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="University of São Tomé and Príncipe">University of São Tomé and Príncipe</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>São Toméan <a href="/wiki/Culture" title="Culture">culture</a> is a mixture of <a href="/wiki/African_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="African culture">African</a> and <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Portuguese culture">Portuguese</a> influences.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Music of São Tomé and Príncipe">Music of São Tomé and Príncipe</a></div>
<p>São Toméans are known for <i>ússua</i> and <i>socopé</i> <a href="/wiki/Rhythm" title="Rhythm">rhythms</a>, while Príncipe is home to the <i>dêxa</i> beat. Portuguese <a href="/wiki/Ballroom" title="Ballroom">ballroom</a> may have played an integral part in the development of these rhythms and their associated dances.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p><i>Tchiloli</i> is a musical dance performance that tells a dramatic story. The danço-Congo is similarly a combination of music, dance, and theatre. <a href="/wiki/Morna_(music)" title="Morna (music)">Morna</a> is a music genre from the islands and <a href="/wiki/Ces%C3%A1ria_%C3%89vora" title="Cesária Évora">Cesária Évora</a> was known as the Queen of Morna.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Literature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Literature_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Literature of São Tomé and Príncipe">Literature of São Tomé and Príncipe</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/List_of_African_writers_by_country#São_Tomé_and_Príncipe" title="List of African writers by country">List of writers from São Tomé and Príncipe</a></div><p>São Tomé and Príncipe's Portuguese-language literature and poetry is considered some of the richest in <a href="/wiki/Portuguese-speaking_African_countries" title="Portuguese-speaking African countries">Lusophone Africa</a>. Other literature from the country has been written in <a href="/wiki/Forro_Creole" title="Forro Creole">Forro Creole</a>, English and Caué Creole. <a href="/wiki/Francisco_Jos%C3%A9_Tenreiro" title="Francisco José Tenreiro">Francisco José Tenreiro</a> is considered one of the country's most influential writers. Other notable literary figures include <a href="/wiki/Manuela_Margarido" title="Manuela Margarido">Manuela Margarido</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alda_Espirito_Santo" class="mw-redirect" title="Alda Espirito Santo">Alda Espirito Santo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Olinda_Beja" title="Olinda Beja">Olinda Beja</a> and <a href="/wiki/Concei%C3%A7%C3%A3o_Lima" title="Conceição Lima">Conceição Lima</a>.
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe" title="Cuisine of São Tomé and Príncipe">Cuisine of São Tomé and Príncipe</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Staple_foods" class="mw-redirect" title="Staple foods">Staple foods</a> include <a href="/wiki/Fish_(food)" class="mw-redirect" title="Fish (food)">fish</a>, <a href="/wiki/Seafood" title="Seafood">seafood</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bean" title="Bean">beans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maize" title="Maize">maize</a>, and cooked <a href="/wiki/Banana" title="Banana">banana</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Evans_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Evans-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Becker_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Becker-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Tropical_fruit" title="Tropical fruit">Tropical fruits</a>, such as <a href="/wiki/Pineapple" title="Pineapple">pineapple</a>, <a href="/wiki/Avocado" title="Avocado">avocado</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Banana" title="Banana">bananas</a>, are  significant components of the cuisine. The use of hot spices is prominent in São Tomése cuisine.<sup id="cite_ref-Evans_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Evans-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Coffee is used in various dishes as a <a href="/wiki/Spice" title="Spice">spice</a> or <a href="/wiki/Seasoning" title="Seasoning">seasoning</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Evans_83-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Evans-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Breakfast dishes are often reheated leftovers from the previous evening's meal, and <a href="/wiki/Omelettes" class="mw-redirect" title="Omelettes">omelettes</a> are popular.<sup id="cite_ref-Becker_84-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Becker-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Football (soccer) is the most famous sport in São Tomé and Principe, the <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe_national_football_team" title="São Tomé and Príncipe national football team">São Tomé and Príncipe national football team</a> is the national <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">association football</a> team of São Tomé and Príncipe and is controlled by the <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9an_Football_Federation" title="São Toméan Football Federation">São Toméan Football Federation</a>. It is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Confederation_of_African_Football" title="Confederation of African Football">Confederation of African Football</a> (CAF) and <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

In 2002, East Timor's independence was fully recognized.</li></ul></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table><div></div></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="navbox-list navbox-odd" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em"></div><table class="nowraplinks mw-collapsible mw-collapsed navbox-subgroup" style="border-spacing:0"><tbody><tr><th scope="col" class="navbox-title" colspan="2" style="background:transparent;text-align:left;"><div id="North_America_&amp;amp;_North_Atlantic" style="font-size:114%;margin:0 4em">North America &amp; North Atlantic</div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="navbox-list navbox-odd" style="width:100%;padding:0;border-top:1px solid #aaa;padding-left:1.0em;text-align:left;border-bottom:1px solid #aaa;"><div style="padding:0 0.25em"><div>
